ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-22.u10	A declaration on behalf of a group!
ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-22.u11	Here you go, Mr. Karadaye.
ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-22.u12	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Dear ladies and gentlemen of the House! On February 21st, we celebrated the International Mother tongue Day. This day was set in 1999 by the UNESCO General Conference in memory of the loss of their lives during a demonstration in 1952, young people who defended the right to use their mother tongue officially. The day marks the importance of learning and using mother tongue and aims to promote multilingualism and cultural diversity, and not only. The idea of the initiative is to stimulate solidarity and tolerance to different things. Among the goals of the initiative is to encourage people to keep their mother tongue but also to enrich their vocabulary with other languages. The preservation and preservation of languages in the world is among the main goals of UNESCO. The organisation accepts linguistic diversity as one of the most powerful tools for preserving mankind's material and intangible heritage. UNESCO actively supports countries that make full use of all forms of communication and knowledge transfer, promote linguistic diversity, finance education in the mother tongue and thus promote cultural diversity as a public value. Language is a fundamental human right and is a fundamental factor for democracy. In our country this is a right laid down in the basic law of the state "The Constitution of the Republic of Bulgaria." However, the right to study and use mother tongue has not yet obtained the relevant legislation in the country's laws. The European pillar of social rights as its first principle states that everyone has the right to access quality and inclusive education, training and lifelong learning in order to maintain and acquire skills that enable every citizen to participate fully in society and successfully realise on the labour market. The Council Recommendation of 22 May 2018 based on the reference framework for key competences in the education of the European Union of 2007 sets out a set of key competences needed for personal realization, health, employability and social inclusion. This set of competences is defined with regard to public and economic development, but also as a result of various initiatives, with particular attention being paid to improving basic skills, investing in language learning. The first competence in all Member States of the European Union is translated as a mother tongue, only in Bulgaria is translated as official. Despite the recommendation that the positive attitude includes assessing cultural diversity and intercultural communication, including respect for the individual language profile of each person, who also includes respect for the mother tongue of persons belonging to minorities, in our country does not simply lack a positive attitude, but directly with a sub-legal legal act of the Council of Ministers, the mother tongue is excluded from the list of general education articles. The state does not finance the issue of the language textbooks. This example only partially shows the breakthroughs of democracy in Bulgaria, the level of human rights and freedoms. Solving this issue is part of the task of general strengthening democracy and establishing the rule of law in Bulgaria, and this is an integral part of the mission of the Movement for Rights and Freedoms. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament, language is rich not only because it is a means of communication, it is identity, cultural diversity and spiritual upliftment. The language is the bond with the mother, the family, the motherland, but it is also the code by which we can get to know and accept the different, become part of his world, but also unite him to ours. The knowledge of more languages is wealth and must be encouraged. Having a good knowledge of the language spoken at home is the basis for all literacy and intelligence. And since our deep understanding is that all Bulgarians in the world should have the opportunity to learn their mother tongue, in the Budget Act we will make a proposal to provide financial resources for them. Mr. Chairman, ladies and gentlemen of the House! According to our Constitution, Bulgarian citizens, no matter what ethnic origins they have, what faith they profess, where they live and what their mother tongue is, have equal rights and the laws of the country must ensure this. We at the Movement for Rights and Freedoms take the International Mother tongue Day as an occasion to mark bitter truths about the problems of minorities in Bulgaria to use their language and to highlight our historic mission to build our common future in our common homeland. We salute everyone with the holiday! We congratulate all teachers in their mother tongues for their dedication and professionalism! We appreciate their work, patriotism and humanism and support their empathy for European ideas and values! We congratulate all parents and children who realize the meaning of the mother tongue, study it, keep it, and thus bequeath it to the future! We also congratulate all cultural figures who keep the traditions of minority communities in Bulgaria as part of our country's national cultural wealth! [[Applause from DPS.]]

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-22.u24	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Dear colleagues, 21 February as the International Mother Language Study Day should remind us that Bulgaria is one of the countries that have the largest historical community on the Balkan Peninsula, and not only on the Balkan Peninsula, which currently numbers more than 2 and a half million people scattered in all neighbouring countries. Bulgarian minorities, some of them recognised and some not fully, have in Romania, Serbia, Kosovo, Albania, Greece, Turkey, Hungary, Slovakia, Moldova, Ukraine and Russia. On this day we must remember not only our compatriots who are cut off from the territory of Bulgaria for one reason or another, but we must also remember that at present the Bulgarian state is in debt to them, because the study of the mother tongue, which is one of the fundamental rights and which our country has given to all its fellow citizens, who have another native language, other than the official Bulgarian language, is not guaranteed and is not given in the same way as we have given it to our fellow citizens with another mother tongue. In Greece, a Member State of the European Union, for nearly 40 years teaching Bulgarian language in schools for the Bulgarian minority, which is not officially recognised, is prohibited. Instead, an artificial pomach language was manufactured there, which began to be taught in the schools of Muslim Bulgarians in northern Greece, in the region of Xanthian and Gumirdzhinsko. In Turkey, where the last census, marking a mother tongue other than Turkish, marked 106,000 people with Bulgarian mother tongue in 1965, which makes the Bulgarians then the fourth largest minority group in our southern neighbour after the Kurds, Arabs and Bushes. At present there are more than 650,000 people living with Bulgarian mother tongue. The Bulgarian population is close to one third of the territory of Eastern Thrace. The Bulgarian language is spoken as the main mother, as spoken on the territory of Lozengradsko, Edirne, Rodostensko, Maligradsko. It is also spoken in Asia Minor in Banderma County, around Kodzhaeli, and in many other places where there are displaced countrymen, Muslim Bulgarians. Unfortunately, our southern neighbour does not guarantee any of the fundamental minority rights that we have given to our compatriots with another mother tongue. Bulgaria here is the time to recall, does not recognise minorities. Ratified by the Bulgarian Parliament in 1999 Framework Convention for the Protection of Minorities Bulgaria notes that there are no such minorities in the territory of our country because the international legal status of the minority is part of a nationality, forced by the mother state, cut off from its own territory. Bulgaria, as is well known, compatriots, borders from everywhere, only and only with Bulgarian lands cut off from its territory. It is right and normal, when we grant rights, to want the same rights for our compatriots in neighbouring countries, and not just in neighbouring countries. In some of them, this issue is settled as an example in Albania, where there is an officially recognised Bulgarian minority. The Bulgarian language is studied and has news in Bulgarian on Albanian television. In Turkey, none of these things are available. There is no study of Bulgarian in Turkish schools, there is no news of Bulgarian on Turkish television, there is no Bulgarian party in Turkey, unlike Bulgaria, where all this is present. We must not forget, and we must never forget that if we do not seek the rights of our countrymen abroad, there is no one to give them. Let us remind ourselves that the minority status given to the Bulgarians in Albania was the result of multilateral pressure from the Bulgarian state, which was one of the nice and pleasant exceptions of Bulgarian foreign policy in the last 30 years as a whole. Dear compatriots, on this day we should all remember that what we have as an absolute right in our country, we Bulgarians, to learn our native language, our countrymen abroad do not have it. These more than 650,000 Bulgarians in Turkey do not have nearly half a million Bulgarians in Turkey! There are not all those nearly 500 thousand Bulgarians in Romania who are scattered throughout the Vlaska plain from Bana to North Dobrudja including, there are no Bulgarians in other territories that have long already, unfortunately, not been in the territory of our country. On February 21st, we are not just celebrating Mother tongue Day. On 21 February we celebrate the day Bulgaria should remember its lost and abandoned compatriots and guarantee them at least the same rights that it gave to those who in Bulgaria have a foreign mother tongue. Let us not forget this: we are all in debt. In debt to future generations, but not only to Bulgaria, but also to future Bulgarian generations outside our country! Thank you. [[Applause from ARGUING.]]

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-22.u108	Dear Mr. Chairman, honourable Members! As I have also pointed out in the written proposal, on the basis of art. 144 of the Rules declare private interest. I declare a private interest as a representative of a guild, currently out of 13 thousand practitioners lawyers, until recently 14 thousand, but 1000 of them have written off in the last one year because of their inability to exercise their profession competitively on the Bulgarian market on the so-called legal services. I have indicated in the written proposal a very detailed motivation why the legally recognised costs should be increased for this profession from 25% to 40%, mainly because our costs really are at least 40%. The costs incurred by lawyers for the exercise of their activities are more than 25% of the costs incurred by lawyers who have chosen the order to be charged under the Law on Taxes and Incomes of Individuals. They are at least 40, as currently legally recognised costs for the income from copyrights, as well as income from the collection of mushrooms and strawberries in the forest. I am in a different line of work, I am not at all concerned, but I would like to point out that the costs of the lawyer's, self-insuring persons are more than 25%, which is why it is appropriate to restore the factual situation as it was until 2007, when the statutory costs of the lawyer's profession were 35%, that is, we will not do anything new. Unfortunately, in the last two years, the legal profession has not received any state protection measures, no financial incentives, no compensation, unlike many other professions, which is why I believe that the increase of the statutory expenditure to their actual level of at least 45% will be a financial aid to them without being considered State aid in the direct sense of the word. Unfortunately, I have heard many debates so far and many opinions in order that if we give the lawyers what we will do with the other free professions. Why shouldn't we give it to them? Why shouldn't we give it to others? Well, I'll tell you at least one difference between the legal profession and the other free professions, which I can think of, and it is precisely that the legal profession helps to carry out the official protection in cases and to provide legal assistance to socially weak and underprivileged citizens. Lawyers dealing with official defences are about 7,000 people from practitioners lawyers, and to the greater extent, not to say all of them practice official protection in the province, outside Sofia, where incomes are generally greater. These 7,000 people, practitioners, self-insuring lawyers, are actually implementing the legal aid that the state provides to the underprivileged and unemployed citizens, those who need legal protection, but cannot afford it. And that's what these fellow self-loathers are doing to us, which are 7,000 - 8,000 people, mostly in smaller towns. Unfortunately, I do not know if you know the payment of this professional protection and special representation in these specific cases is of an extremely minimal amount, which is why as a result the monthly income of our fellow lawyers is an average of between BGN 1500 and BGN 1600. income per month. I have said this in both the Legal and Budget Committee, and in the proposal I have indicated that the costs that we should do, which we are obliged by law to do, such as maintaining an office, paying insurance, raising the qualification, maintaining a collaborator, material and technical base to maintain, are very much above 25%, and on the other hand, we have one minimum and under the Ordinance on minimum legal fees that we cannot fall under in order to be competitive on the market, we have minimums that we need to follow, one part of you knows it. Therefore, it is obtained that for these colleagues, who are several thousand people who carry out the official protection and support the legal function of the state, providing legal assistance to underprivileged and unemployed citizens, they are the lowest-paid people in our guild and they deserve this support the most. Again, I'm not doing it for myself, it's not a political decision. This will be a solution to help this guild, which is a distinction between it and the other professions that I have heard should also be helped. But in the end, if any other profession wants to increase its statutory costs, I want to go out and protect them. I can do it for the guild that I represent here at the National Assembly as a representative of the National Assembly. I therefore ask you, honourable Members, let us support this proposal. We do not do it for the opposition, we do not do it as a political decision, we do it simply so as to ensure the least legal function of the state and to ensure the protection of the poorest part of the population. Otherwise, it will continue this tendency to write off lawyers, including service lawyers, and from 13 000 they will decrease further, which I guarantee you will create problems in the judiciary as well. It is inevitable because no other profession, unfortunately, other than lawyers, can appear to protect citizens who need legal protection. So, please, support him. Thank you.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-22.u116	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Dear colleagues! Out of a total of 13,000 lawyers in the country, 10,000 are exercising the legal profession independently and the proposed change also applies to them. The proposed changes will not affect the revenue in the budget from direct tax from law firms, where in fact large revenue from legal activities is formed, as taxation for them retains its general tax character and tax regime under the CCC. In previous estimates, the average declared income of these 10,000 lawyers is estimated to be around 200 million. lv., or 1,600 lv. monthly. So the proposed change would only affect the budget by about 3 million. lv. Legally, the lawyer is required to have a firm, which includes rent, electricity, water, supplies, professional liability insurance. The lawyer is obliged to increase his qualifications, for which he also owes payment, at least for the use of legal information systems. The lawyer also owes payment to social and health insurance. These real costs significantly exceed those legally recognised. However, self-employed lawyers are not entitled to recognition of real costs. Of all the free professions in Bulgaria, the lawyers remained one of the few who did not receive support from the state in the context of the crisis, although for the period of the so-called Lockdown in 2020, when the courts were not working. Lawyers were completely deprived of income. The work of lawyers stops or sharply decreases due to the suspension of the work of companies and individuals. In one year the number of lawyers in Bulgaria has decreased by 1000 people, one of the main reasons is the sharp reduction in incomes, and the reduction in the number of lawyers leads to the deprivation of citizens and legal persons from qualified legal aid. The problem particularly affects cities outside Sofia, where 8,000 out of 10,000 lawyers are registered, which would also affect the change. The reduction of revenue in the budget by 3 million. lv. it is very likely to be compensated by the preservation of the activities of many lawyers who would otherwise be forced to stop their activities. The protection of the interests of natural and legal persons, guaranteeing the right to a fair trial is impossible without the participation of lawyers and without them is, of course, impossible and attaining the rule of law, which we talk so much about in this Chamber and is spoken about in the Bulgarian society. Therefore, my fellow Members, I call on you to support this change. Thank you. [[Applause from GERB-SDS.]]

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-22.u196	Dear Mr. Chairman, honourable Members of the National Assembly! We have been repeatedly reminded by the media and by this tribune that this budget is social, that this budget is provided for the most deprived. However, when we see what is foreseen in the costs, at least I see that the most damaging are those who are the poorest. My proposal and a group of Members of the National Revival are regarding an increase in the amount for a total dental prosthesis, as well as for products that are related to its development. I would stress that at least 30 thousand people in a year need these dentures, and just those who are the poorest, cannot afford it. No pensioner can afford those pensions he receives to pay his own quality product. Many Bulgarian pensioners cannot eat not only because of the miserable pensions, but also because with these pensions they cannot afford quality dental assistance. I would also stress that even those who are health-insured, the Health Insurance Fund covers very few services for a dental specialist. That is why we need to pay more attention to this problem. Therefore, I propose to provide funds that are only 0,1% of the budget of the National Health Fund for those people who cannot afford to buy their own dentures. These 6 million are nothing compared to the money given for other activities, but will allow 30 thousand Bulgarian pensioners, as well as extremely needy to be able to continue to eat and be able to have a quality life. In the budget, this feather is missing at all. At the same time, the prices of both electricity and products and the materials from which they are made are increased. That is why my proposal is, and I ask you very much to support it, because this will help thousands of people who are most affected by the crisis. Thank you.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-22.u336	Dear Mr. Chairman, dear Minister, on this very interesting day for you, honourable Members of the House! On the basis of art. 80, para. 2 sentence 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly I am making the following proposal for an editorial amendment in the final version of art. 5. In Article 4 (1) (b), point (c) is replaced by the following: 5, para. 1 point 3 shall be amended as follows: the nurses, midwives and associated medical professionals within the meaning of Paragraph 1 (1) of the Word Organizations Act; nurses, midwives and associated dentists and assistant pharmacists in the amount of BGN 360. As motives for this editorial proposal, I can mention the following: the proposed version avoids the existence of a ambiguous interpretation of the norm and clearly defines the circle of persons who are entitled to receive additional wage of BGN 360. in 2022, as well as ensuring that those funds are received from all persons who received them in 2021. The logic of the construction of the provision under art. 5, para. 1, t. 1, 2, 4 and 5 in which the professions of persons entitled to receive such remuneration are specifically mentioned. Our editorial is writtenly agreed with the Ministry of Health and the National Health Insurance Fund. The opinions received by both institutions express support for it without comments, stating that it thus overcomes the confusion and the ambiguous interpretation of the Arrangement and clearly and accurately defines the persons who are entitled to receive additional remuneration. It is indicated that it is also ensured that all persons to whom additional remuneration was paid in 2021 in that order will continue to receive those funds in 2022 as well. The above is apparent from the opinion of Professor Salchev, due to the fact that in 2021, with the rules adopted by the NHIF on the terms and conditions of payment of funds to hospital and medical care contractors, additional remuneration of medical staff for the period of the declared exceptional epidemic situation due to the epidemic spread of contagious disease under art. 61, para. 1 of the Health Act with Section I.1.2 thereof, the NHIF has incorrectly aligned professionals from the professional field with the concept of health care professionals. Thus, in 2021, they were excluded from receiving additional remuneration as assistant pharmacists, which according to art. 1, para. 1 and 2 of the Ordinance on the uniform state requirements for the acquisition of higher education in specialties from the professional field of health and health care and at the same time are included the professional Bachelor and the relevant professional qualification in the specialty of the professional department of health and health care and, in our opinion, the professions of the professional Bachelor and the Orthopedic technician who are not from the professional field of health care are included in the course of 2021, according to us, it is not necessary for them to be deprived of this right for 2022. These are our motives for doing this revision and we propose that it be voted on. Thank you.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-22.u474	Thank you, Mr. Sabrutev. I am opening a discussion on the paragraphs presented. Professor Tonev wanted the floor, and then Professor Angelov.
ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-22.u475	Dear Mr. Chairman, dear Minister, ladies and gentlemen of the House! I'll make a statement under Paragraph 17. The proposed version referred to in point (c) shall aim to fill the legal gap with regard to the adoption of a methodology for the implementation of the mechanism by the Supervisory Board of the Cashier. At present, this methodology governs basic parameters in the implementation of the mechanism, but for its adoption there is no legal delegation in art. 45, para. 31 of the Health Insurance Act. Its acceptance and its application without explicit legal basis therefore leads to questioning its legality. With the creation of letters................................................................................................... Paragraph 2 of Paragraph 17 shall aim at overcoming the regulatory gap with regard to the mechanism ensuring predictability and sustainability of the NHIF's budget for medical devices in art. 45 of the Health Insurance Act. At present, nowhere in the Law provides for how this mechanism applies to medical devices, there is no provision that provides for the signing of contracts between the NHIF and producers, their authorised representatives of medical devices and wholesalers of medical devices. In this regard, the NHIF has no legal basis at present to require the conclusion of such contracts, which could hinder both the legality and the application of the mechanism with regard to medical devices. The proposal lays down the rules on the treatment of medicinal products, a procedure for which groups of medical devices the mechanism for signing contracts with the entities concerned and the deadlines for execution applies. The aim of our proposal is to introduce a clear and unambiguous regulation at the level of the law regarding medical devices paid by the NHIF outside the value of the medical service provided in order to make the process transparent, to have predictability and to protect the interests and rights of the NHIF, economic operators and especially Bulgarian citizens. When examining this proposal by the Working Group before a second vote in the Committee on Budget and Finance, it was initially adopted by the NHIF, but then arguments came forward against. These arguments were made clear by Prof. Salchev about the part about partially paid medical devices, with reasons that the NHIF pays them only to hospital care facilities and the order of negotiation is different from that of the producers and traders who are paid for the fully paid medical devices. To the best of my knowledge, the NHIF has also applied mechanisms for control and compliance with the budgetary framework for partially paid products, but at present they are governed by regulations of the NHIF under the Supervisory Board. I would like to point out that this other line for partially paid medical devices should also be regulated in order to have clear and transparent conditions, order and criteria, which can be done in the contracts with hospitals. I believe that it is appropriate to settle this mechanism in the LDC or Annex as a requirement for the terms and conditions for the conclusion of contracts with hospital care institutions. I hope that the NHIF will take the necessary action for these proposals to be implemented in the shortest possible time. Our proposal in § 17 and its related para. 3 in § 5 it intended that, at the beginning of the year, concessions on the principle of medicines should be negotiated with manufacturers and traders, within the framework of the budget for 2022, in excess of the estimated amounts of the product group concerned, concessions should be granted, i.e. to recover funds in relation to the amount of excess and the principle is similar to that of the medicines. We withdraw our proposal in the part of the products in order not to create tension in the system, but we strongly state that the legislation must have the same rules in the implementation of the mechanism ensuring predictability and sustainability of the NHIF's budget for medicinal products and for fully paid medical devices in the context of hospital medical care. Thank you.

But that's one small detail. What we're offering. Since the average social security income in recent years is growing rapidly, unlike European countries, for example, in Bulgaria in the last 10 years it has grown nearly twice and something. While inflation and this harmonised index of consumer prices in recent years are somewhere around 1 - 2% and so on. So we're proposing a change in the ratio. I know how dynamic these processes are, but this year, in any case, inflation is expected to be double-digit and not the way the government suggests at the end of the year. That is why we propose to change the ratio for at least this year - 75% of the increase in insurance income and 25% of inflation growth. This new structure of the Swiss rule would give an increase, updating pensions by 7.7% instead of these 6.1%. The cost is minimal, so consider, honourable Members, whether it is normal and it is logical to support such a proposal, which we think is reasonable.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-22.u846	Dear Mr. Chairman, honourable Members, Dear Mr. Minister! The proposal that we have made in § 6 concerns the dropping of the ceiling of pensions and the reasons for this proposal are as follows: the provisions of the Social Security Code lay down the method for determining the size of pensions. The amounts of pensions related to the employment of persons shall be determined in particular by reference to the insurance service and the insurance income, i.e., above all, to the contribution to the provision. In this respect, it is unfair that there is an artificial restriction on the actual size of the pensions of persons with a significant contribution to the provision. I would like to remind you that these individuals were about 20 thousand, and with the recent changes made on 25 December, there are now 34,000 people. Their number will continue to grow in the future, so surely, given that a decision has been made to increase the maximum social security income, it is also appropriate to make a decision to drop the ceiling so that people are not demotivated to provide themselves and pay insurance to their real income. Moreover, the fact that more and more Bulgarian citizens are being provided abroad and are looking for other alternative ways of the pension system by insurance or investment should also be taken into account. It has already been mentioned in previous discussions that the percentage of dependence on the budget of state social security is increasing, becoming almost 42% this year. This is dangerous because we have struggled for many years to go in the opposite direction, and now we have an opposite trend, because this puts the social system at risk accordingly. We should not continue to violate trust in the pension system, especially with regard to social contributions. If you are going to go to a formula that constantly increases the maximum insurance income that you intend to declare here from the stands, then you must go to a smooth drop of the ceiling. In this case, since it has already been mentioned several times, including by the Minister of Labour and Social Policy in his interviews, including by the President of Parliament's Labour and Social Policy Commission Mr. Arabadzhiev, in interviews it was also mentioned that the ceiling should be dropped, we do not see what serious arguments this ceiling should not be dropped right now, but not to wait 1 July. Given that we have done the appropriate calculations, I would like to inform you that this proposal would mean 10 million. lv. The Commission therefore concludes that the measures provided for in this Decision are in accordance with the opinion of the Committee established by Article 108 (3) of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union and that they are compatible with the internal market within the meaning of Article 108 (3) of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. lv. a month. That's what the offer would be worth. We don't think there's any point in waiting until July 1st. Instead of continuing to demotivate people to provide themselves with their real income, let's take this step right now. Thank you.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-22.u880	Dear Mr. Chairman, honourable Members! Dear Minister, I will present to you the arguments regarding the proposal that we made by the GERB-SDS parliamentary group at the first discussion of the Draft Budget Bill for State Social Security for 2022, namely in the case of the non-use of the additional paid leave for raising a child up to 2 years of age, the corresponding monetary compensation shall be 100% of the amount of compensation received by the mother in the second year. However, here is the time, Mr. Chairman, to make a proposal on the grounds of art. 80, para. 2 of the Rules on Organization and Activity of the National Assembly for Editorial Amendment, and I propose that instead of 100% read 70%, I will also make my justification for this. Our proposal was dictated in order to return to the labour market more quickly and encourage the combination of parents' personal and professional lives, with fiscal motives, of course, important, but not leading. The proposal to change the amount of money compensation for children's mothers up to 2 years of age paid leave should be considered from different points of view, but here the main and leading point of view that we need to consider is that of mothers and what is the benefit for them from increasing the amount of this compensation. First of all, the income of the family is increased, because if the mother returns to work, she will receive the full amount of her salary plus 70% of the amount of the money compensation, which is valued compared to BGN 710, which is the amount for the second year, is equal to BGN 497. Secondly, the return to work before the age of 2 years of age of the child, combined with the increased amount of money compensation, will also provide relative financial stability to the family and the ability of the mother and parents to hire a babysitter at all to care for the child in the absence of seats in the nursery or to close relatives to care for the child, so that this compensation can serve as financial support. Here we have to bear in mind, honourable Members, that the project "Parents in Employment" under the Operational Programme "Human Resources Development" which was in operation until last year, until 1 June 2021, is no longer operational. And under this project, parents had the opportunity to hire babysitters, with the salary of the babysitters being paid from the project budget. Unfortunately, this project is no longer operational. There is no alternative to such a programme to be proposed by either the two official governments or the regular government to be equivalent and to enable babysitters to be financed from the state budget. Thirdly, mothers working in a competitive environment and growing up in the profession are constantly developing and improving technological and digital skills will be encouraged to return to work faster so that they do not lose their qualifications and opportunities for development. It is important to point out that long stay outside the labour market makes it difficult to adapt at work and is a prerequisite for the loss of working habits. And fourthly, long stay in motherhood also leads to a loss of rights for insured mothers. During the last 20 years, there has been a startling trend in the labour market related to the ageing of the workforce, on the one hand, and on the other, with a shortage of labour. According to preliminary data from the National Insurance Institute for 2021 from 70 254 mothers who raise their children up to 2 years of age, 15 154 are returned before the child reaches 2 years of work, which means that if the rate of monetary compensation is increased from 50 to 70%, total funds of 67 million will have to be allocated to the State budget. Seven hundred and eighty-three thousand. And a little extra leva. At the same time, however, again according to preliminary data of the National Insurance Institute, at BGN 1650. average salary in Bulgaria, the return on the state budget from the insurance at the expense of the employer and the worker will amount to 74 million. 262 thousand. lv. In other words, there will be higher income from insurance than expenditure on cash benefits. In this sense, colleagues, I ask you to reconsider and really make an estimate of the fact that when mothers return to work, there will be a higher contribution to the budget, unlike when money benefits are paid. There are different models in the European Union. We have adopted this model, but obviously we do not want to help employers. Not in the budget, in the Social Security Code in the Cross-border provision. The golden Swiss rule implies the sum of the increase in insurance income and 50% of inflation growth. Because in Bulgaria the average insurance income, as we started from low levels, the economy started to develop, is growing very quickly. Over the past ten years, more than twice the average social security income has risen, and inflation has been at levels between one and two percent. I'm talking about the harmonised consumer price index, the big consumer basket.
